I also couldn't run the game here, so the fix is not confirmed in play.\n\n**Ship: `data/models/ships/skipjack/skipjack_low.dae`**\n- `tag_landing` marks where the ship's landing gear touches the pad. The game uses it to set the ship's height when docked.\n- In the low-detail version it was at z = **-3.860857**. The medium and high-detail versions, the Skipjack collision mesh and the gear all sit at **-4.31**.\n- The game uses this bad value because it loads the lowest-detail version first and keeps the first tag with a given name. As a result a docked Skipjack sat 0.45 m too low, with its gear inside the pad.\n\n**Station: `data/models/stations/new_ground/new_ground_landing.dae`**\n- The invisible collision surface for pad 1 (`collision_pad1`) was at z = **73.30775**. The other five pads are at **72.83775**, and so is the visible pad surface.\n- So pad 1's collision surface was an invisible layer 0.47 m above what you see.\n\n**How this causes the damage:** together, these left a docked Skipjack's hull about 1.2 m inside pad 1's collision surface. As soon as the ship starts to leave, it is already overlapping a surface the station no longer reserves for it. The game counts that as a collision and damages the hull, even though the ship visibly hasn't touched anything. Both values are now back in line with the matching data, so the ship should sit on top of the pad surface on every pad and leave at 100% hull.\n\nEach change is a single number, and nothing else in the ships or stations was touched. I also found no saved compiled copies of these models that could still hold the old values.\n\nThe two values look like deliberate edits: both differ from the matching data by a round amount with the same trailing digits. I checked every ship's landing tag and every station's pads and found no other mismatch.
